Yeah, actually. I used to do a lot of things. Like, I’d plan these big Sunday brunches for my friends, or I'd spend hours painting in my spare room. I even used to go hiking on the weekends and sign up for all those professional networking events. But now... it all just feels like a list of chores I'm pretending to care about. Speaking of the spare room, I was trying to organize it the other day, and I found this old box of hiking boots. I bought them when I went to Colorado three years ago. No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed most of the time. I mean, I have those feelings of guilt, like I said, but it's not this constant, heavy sadness every single day. I still have okay days where I just go about my routine. Like yesterday, I went to the grocery store after work. I usually go to the one on 4th Street because they have a much better produce section, especially their avocados. The ones at the store closer to my house are always either rock hard or completely mushy inside. I tried making guacamole last week for a potluck, and I had to throw out three whole avocados because they were just brown and stringy when I cut into them. It's so frustrating because they're not cheap, you know? Anyway, the potluck was for my neighbor's retirement. He used to be a mail carrier, and he always used to leave the packages right on my porch swing instead of by the door so they wouldn't get wet if it rained. He's moving to Arizona now to be closer to his grandkids. I think he said Phoenix, or maybe Tucson? I can't remember. But yeah, to answer your question, no, I don't feel down all day every day.

Yeah, actually, I have noticed that. Especially at work in the mornings. It’s like... I just move so slowly. They had this beautiful golden retriever that just wanted to play fetch the whole time I was there. I've always wanted a dog, but my HOA has all these strict rules about pets over a certain weight limit, which just seems so unfair. But anyway, yeah, I just feel like I'm failing at everything and it's all my fault.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once found pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. I used to do a lot of things. Like, I’d plan these big Sunday brunches for my friends, or I'd spend hours painting in my spare room. I even used to go hiking on the weekends and sign up for all those professional networking events. But now... it all just feels like a list of chores I'm pretending to care about. Speaking of the spare room, I was trying to organize it the other day, and I found this old box of hiking boots. I bought them when I went to Colorado three years ago. The mountains there are just incredible, you know? The air is so thin, though. I remember getting lightheaded on the first day. We stayed in this little cabin that had a wood-burning stove, and it took me forever to figure out how to light it without smoking out the whole place. The host was this guy named Greg, who made his own maple syrup. But yeah, I just don't really care about any of that stuff anymore. It's just exhausting to even think about.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired or having less energy lately, even when you're not doing much?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. Like, physically, my energy is fine. I haven't noticed being more tired than usual. I still get up and go to the office every day. Actually, they just replaced the coffee machine in the breakroom at work. It used to be one of those standard drip ones, but now it's this fancy espresso maker that takes these little aluminum pods. My coworker, Sarah—she sits in the cubicle next to me—she was complaining that the pods are bad for the environment. Which, I guess they are, but she drives this massive SUV to work every day, so it feels a little hypocritical, you know? She brought in her own reusable pod thing, but it spilled wet coffee grounds all over the counter on Tuesday. I ended up having to wipe it all up with those terrible, cheap brown paper towels they stock in the dispenser. They basically just push the water around instead of absorbing it. But, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, energy. No, I don't think my energy levels have really dropped or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the time, nearly every day, not just occasionally?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed most of the time. I mean, I have those feelings of guilt, like I said, but it's not this constant, heavy sadness every single day. I still have okay days where I just go about my routine. Like yesterday, I went to the grocery store after work. I usually go to the one on 4th Street because they have a much better produce section, especially their avocados. The ones at the store closer to my house are always either rock hard or completely mushy inside. I tried making guacamole last week for a potluck, and I had to throw out three whole avocados because they were just brown and stringy when I cut into them. It's so frustrating because they're not cheap, you know? Anyway, the potluck was for my neighbor's retirement. He used to be a mail carrier, and he always used to leave the packages right on my porch swing instead of by the door so they wouldn't get wet if it rained. He's moving to Arizona now to be closer to his grandkids. I think he said Phoenix, or maybe Tucson? I can't remember. But yeah, to answer your question, no, I don't feel down all day every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more than before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have noticed that. Especially at work in the mornings. It’s like... I just move so slowly. Opening up my spreadsheets, typing out responses to emails, or even just walking down the hall to a meeting—it feels like I’m physically pushing my way through wet cement. Speaking of cement, they’re doing all this construction on the road right outside my apartment building. They started tearing up the sidewalk like three weeks ago, and the noise is just unbelievable. They have these massive jackhammers going at like 7:00 AM. I tried wearing earplugs, but the foam ones always fall out of my ears while I'm sleeping. I even bought those silicone ones that you mold to your ear, but they felt so weird and sticky, and I kept worrying they were going to melt into my ear canal or something. My landlord sent out an email saying the construction would be done by the end of the month, but you know how city projects are. Last year they said they were fixing the pipes under the street and it took them six months. The water pressure in my shower was terrible the whole time. I had to buy this special showerhead just to get enough pressure to wash the shampoo out of my hair. But, um... yeah, the slow moving thing. Yeah, I... I actually came in because I just can't stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault. It's like this constant weight. I feel like I'm a terrible employee, and honestly, a bad homeowner too. I just feel like I deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for. Not at all what the little swatch at the hardware store looked like. The guy at the paint counter mixed it right in front of me, but I think their lighting in the store is just really misleading. They have those harsh fluorescent bulbs everywhere. I remember reading an article once about how fluorescent lighting can actually trigger migraines for some people. My cousin gets terrible migraines, the kind where she has to lie in a dark room with a cold washcloth over her eyes for hours. She tried acupuncture for it once, but she said it just made her feel anxious having all those little needles stuck in her. I don't think I could ever do acupuncture. The idea of needles just freaks me out too much. When I go to the dentist for a filling, I have to close my eyes so I don't see the needle for the novocaine. My dentist is really nice about it though, he always plays classic rock in the background to distract me. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just feel like I'm failing at everything, letting everyone down, even if they don't say it.

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, I mean... it hasn't just been a short period. This whole feeling, the depression and just not caring about anything I used to like... it's been going on for about two years and one month now. It feels like such a specific amount of time because it started right around the time I bought my car. It's a Honda Civic, silver. I originally wanted the blue one, but the dealership didn't have any on the lot with the trim package I wanted, and they said it would take six weeks to order one in. I couldn't wait that long because my old car—this beat-up Toyota Corolla—had just completely died on me on the highway. The transmission just gave out right in the middle of rush hour traffic. It was terrifying. I had to wait on the shoulder for a tow truck for two hours in the freezing cold. The tow truck driver was this really chatty guy named Earl, and he told me all about his daughter who was studying to be a marine biologist in Florida. He showed me pictures of her swimming with manatees. Manatees are so strange looking, aren't they? Like giant underwater potatoes. I watched a documentary about them once, and apparently, they get injured by boat propellers all the time because they swim so close to the surface. It's really sad. They just seem so gentle. But yeah, anyway, the car... I've had it for just over two years, which is exactly how long I've been feeling this way. Two years and a month of just... dragging myself through everything.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before the two years? Well, honestly, looking back at this whole thing... the low mood and the loss of interest, they've been there for almost the whole time since this started two years ago. I've really only had these super brief periods where I could function a little better. Like, I had this one weekend a few months ago where I actually felt okay enough to reorganize my home office. I bought this huge modular desk organizer from IKEA. It was one of those ones with a million tiny wooden pegs, you know? I swear IKEA puts extra pegs in the bag just to mess with you. I always end up with three left over and completely panic that my shelf is going to collapse. 

My brother actually tried to build an IKEA bed frame once without looking at the instructions, and it completely fell apart the first night he slept on it. He had to sleep on the mattress on the floor for a month because he was too stubborn to admit he did it wrong. He's always been like that, even when we were kids. He once refused to wear a winter coat because he said it restricted his arms, so he just shivered at the bus stop every morning. Our mom was furious, she kept saying he was going to catch pneumonia. 

But, um... what was the question? Oh, right. Yeah, before the two years, I don't really know, but since this all started, it's just been that constant low mood almost the entire time, minus those really brief windows where I can actually get something done.

I used to do a lot of things. Like, I’d plan these big Sunday brunches for my friends, or I'd spend hours painting in my spare room. Speaking of the spare room, I was trying to organize it the other day, and I found this old box of hiking boots. I bought them when I went to Colorado three years ago. The mountains there are just incredible, you know? The air is so thin, though. I remember getting lightheaded on the first day. We stayed in this little cabin that had a wood-burning stove, and it took me forever to figure out how to light it without smoking out the whole place. The host was this guy named Greg, who made his own maple syrup. But yeah, I just don't really care about any of that stuff anymore. Actually, they just replaced the coffee machine in the breakroom at work. It used to be one of those standard drip ones, but now it's this fancy espresso maker that takes these little aluminum pods. My coworker, Sarah—she sits in the cubicle next to me—she was complaining that the pods are bad for the environment. Which, I guess they are, but she drives this massive SUV to work every day, so it feels a little hypocritical, you know? The ones at the store closer to my house are always either rock hard or completely mushy inside. I tried making guacamole last week for a potluck, and I had to throw out three whole avocados because they were just brown and stringy when I cut into them. It's so frustrating because they're not cheap, you know? Anyway, the potluck was for my neighbor's retirement. He used to be a mail carrier, and he always used to leave the packages right on my porch swing instead of by the door so they wouldn't get wet if it rained. He's moving to Arizona now to be closer to his grandkids. I think he said Phoenix, or maybe Tucson? I can't remember. But yeah, to answer your question, no, I don't feel down all day every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more than before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have noticed that. Especially at work in the mornings. It’s like... I just move so slowly. Opening up my spreadsheets, typing out responses to emails, or even just walking down the hall to a meeting—it feels like I’m physically pushing my way through wet cement. Speaking of cement, they’re doing all this construction on the road right outside my apartment building. They started tearing up the sidewalk like three weeks ago, and the noise is just unbelievable. They have these massive jackhammers going at like 7:00 AM. I tried wearing earplugs, but the foam ones always fall out of my ears while I'm sleeping. I even bought those silicone ones that you mold to your ear, but they felt so weird and sticky, and I kept worrying they were going to melt into my ear canal or something. My landlord sent out an email saying the construction would be done by the end of the month, but you know how city projects are. Last year they said they were fixing the pipes under the street and it took them six months. The water pressure in my shower was terrible the whole time. I had to buy this special showerhead just to get enough pressure to wash the shampoo out of my hair. But, um... yeah, the slow moving thing. It's definitely happening, mostly just feeling really heavy and slow in the mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re a failure or that you’ve let people down, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I... I actually came in because I just can't stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault. It's like this constant weight. I feel like I'm a terrible employee, and honestly, a bad homeowner too. I just feel like I deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for. Speaking of the house, I finally got around to painting the guest bedroom last weekend. I picked out this color called \"Seafoam Mist,\" but when it dried, it looked way more like a bright mint green. Not at all what the little swatch at the hardware store looked like. The guy at the paint counter mixed it right in front of me, but I think their lighting in the store is just really misleading. They have those harsh fluorescent bulbs everywhere. I remember reading an article once about how fluorescent lighting can actually trigger migraines for some people. My cousin gets terrible migraines, the kind where she has to lie in a dark room with a cold washcloth over her eyes for hours. She tried acupuncture for it once, but she said it just made her feel anxious having all those little needles stuck in her. I don't think I could ever do acupuncture. The idea of needles just freaks me out too much. When I go to the dentist for a filling, I have to close my eyes so I don't see the needle for the novocaine. My dentist is really nice about it though, he always plays classic rock in the background to distract me. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just feel like I'm failing at everything, letting everyone down, even if they don't say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period like this before, where you felt like you were failing at everything and moving through life more slowly?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I mean... it hasn't just been a short period. This whole feeling, the depression and just not caring about anything I used to like... it's been going on for about two years and one month now. It feels like such a specific amount of time because it started right around the time I bought my car. It's a Honda Civic, silver. I originally wanted the blue one, but the dealership didn't have any on the lot with the trim package I wanted, and they said it would take six weeks to order one in. I couldn't wait that long because my old car—this beat-up Toyota Corolla—had just completely died on me on the highway. The transmission just gave out right in the middle of rush hour traffic. It was terrifying. I had to wait on the shoulder for a tow truck for two hours in the freezing cold. The tow truck driver was this really chatty guy named Earl, and he told me all about his daughter who was studying to be a marine biologist in Florida. He showed me pictures of her swimming with manatees. Manatees are so strange looking, aren't they? Like giant underwater potatoes. I watched a documentary about them once, and apparently, they get injured by boat propellers all the time because they swim so close to the surface. It's really sad. They just seem so gentle. But yeah, anyway, the car... I've had it for just over two years, which is exactly how long I've been feeling this way. I always end up with three left over and completely panic that my shelf is going to collapse. \n\nMy brother actually tried to build an IKEA bed frame once without looking at the instructions, and it completely fell apart the first night he slept on it. He had to sleep on the mattress on the floor for a month because he was too stubborn to admit he did it wrong. He's always been like that, even when we were kids. He once refused to wear a winter coat because he said it restricted his arms, so he just shivered at the bus stop every morning. Our mom was furious, she kept saying he was going to catch pneumonia. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right. Yeah, before the two years, I don't really know, but since this all started, it's just been that constant low mood almost the entire time, minus those really brief windows where I can actually get something done.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a time during this two-year period where your mood was unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you had much more energy than usual, or maybe your thoughts were racing and you didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Elevated? I thought maybe if I learned how to make latte art, it would give me something to look forward to in the morning. I tried to make a heart the other day, but it just looked like a blob. My friend Sarah is a barista, and she makes these incredible swans and ferns in the foam. She works at this little independent coffee shop downtown, the one with the exposed brick walls and all the hanging plants. They sell these amazing blueberry scones there. I tried baking scones once, but I overworked the dough and they came out hard as rocks. You're supposed to keep the butter really cold when you make scones, like, freezing cold. Some people even grate frozen butter into the flour. I saw that on a baking competition show. Well, um... there was this one time, yeah. It was a stretch of maybe a few weeks where the heaviness kind of lifted a bit. I was actually getting through my work at the office more normally, answering emails without staring at the screen for an hour first. Opening up my spreadsheets, typing out responses to emails, or even just walking down the hall to a meeting—it feels like I’m physically pushing my way through wet cement. Speaking of cement, they’re doing all this construction on the road right outside my apartment building. They started tearing up the sidewalk like three weeks ago, and the noise is just unbelievable. They have these massive jackhammers going at like 7:00 AM. I tried wearing earplugs, but the foam ones always fall out of my ears while I'm sleeping. I even bought those silicone ones that you mold to your ear, but they felt so weird and sticky, and I kept worrying they were going to melt into my ear canal or something. My landlord sent out an email saying the construction would be done by the end of the month, but you know how city projects are. Last year they said they were fixing the pipes under the street and it took them six months. The water pressure in my shower was terrible the whole time. I had to buy this special showerhead just to get enough pressure to wash the shampoo out of my hair. But, um... yeah, the slow moving thing. It's definitely happening, mostly just feeling really heavy and slow in the mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re a failure or that you’ve let people down, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I... I actually came in because I just can't stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault. It's like this constant weight. I feel like I'm a terrible employee, and honestly, a bad homeowner too. I just feel like I deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for. Speaking of the house, I finally got around to painting the guest bedroom last weekend. I picked out this color called \"Seafoam Mist,\" but when it dried, it looked way more like a bright mint green. Not at all what the little swatch at the hardware store looked like. The guy at the paint counter mixed it right in front of me, but I think their lighting in the store is just really misleading. They have those harsh fluorescent bulbs everywhere. I remember reading an article once about how fluorescent lighting can actually trigger migraines for some people. My cousin gets terrible migraines, the kind where she has to lie in a dark room with a cold washcloth over her eyes for hours. She tried acupuncture for it once, but she said it just made her feel anxious having all those little needles stuck in her. I don't think I could ever do acupuncture. The idea of needles just freaks me out too much. When I go to the dentist for a filling, I have to close my eyes so I don't see the needle for the novocaine. My dentist is really nice about it though, he always plays classic rock in the background to distract me. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just feel like I'm failing at everything, letting everyone down, even if they don't say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period like this before, where you felt like you were failing at everything and moving through life more slowly?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I mean... it hasn't just been a short period. This whole feeling, the depression and just not caring about anything I used to like... it's been going on for about two years and one month now. It feels like such a specific amount of time because it started right around the time I bought my car. It's a Honda Civic, silver. I originally wanted the blue one, but the dealership didn't have any on the lot with the trim package I wanted, and they said it would take six weeks to order one in. I couldn't wait that long because my old car—this beat-up Toyota Corolla—had just completely died on me on the highway. The transmission just gave out right in the middle of rush hour traffic. It was terrifying. I had to wait on the shoulder for a tow truck for two hours in the freezing cold. The tow truck driver was this really chatty guy named Earl, and he told me all about his daughter who was studying to be a marine biologist in Florida. He showed me pictures of her swimming with manatees. Manatees are so strange looking, aren't they? Like giant underwater potatoes. I watched a documentary about them once, and apparently, they get injured by boat propellers all the time because they swim so close to the surface. It's really sad. They just seem so gentle. But yeah, anyway, the car... I've had it for just over two years, which is exactly how long I've been feeling this way. Two years and a month of just... dragging myself through everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, even before this current two-year period?\n\nPatient: Before the two years? Well, honestly, looking back at this whole thing... the low mood and the loss of interest, they've been there for almost the whole time since this started two years ago. I've really only had these super brief periods where I could function a little better. Like, I had this one weekend a few months ago where I actually felt okay enough to reorganize my home office. I bought this huge modular desk organizer from IKEA. It was one of those ones with a million tiny wooden pegs, you know? I swear IKEA puts extra pegs in the bag just to mess with you. I always end up with three left over and completely panic that my shelf is going to collapse. \n\nMy brother actually tried to build an IKEA bed frame once without looking at the instructions, and it completely fell apart the first night he slept on it. He had to sleep on the mattress on the floor for a month because he was too stubborn to admit he did it wrong. He's always been like that, even when we were kids. Our mom was furious, she kept saying he was going to catch pneumonia. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right. Yeah, before the two years, I don't really know, but since this all started, it's just been that constant low mood almost the entire time, minus those really brief windows where I can actually get something done.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a time during this two-year period where your mood was unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you had much more energy than usual, or maybe your thoughts were racing and you didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Elevated? Like, having too much energy? No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt energetic at all. Honestly, it's a struggle just to get out of bed most days. I thought maybe if I learned how to make latte art, it would give me something to look forward to in the morning. I tried to make a heart the other day, but it just looked like a blob. My friend Sarah is a barista, and she makes these incredible swans and ferns in the foam. She works at this little independent coffee shop downtown, the one with the exposed brick walls and all the hanging plants. They sell these amazing blueberry scones there. I tried baking scones once, but I overworked the dough and they came out hard as rocks. You're supposed to keep the butter really cold when you make scones, like, freezing cold. Some people even grate frozen butter into the flour. I saw that on a baking competition show. I love those shows, it's so relaxing to watch people stress over spun sugar while I'm just sitting on my couch. But, um... no, I haven't had any racing thoughts or needed less sleep. Just the opposite, really.\n\nDoctor: Between these times when you’ve felt down and slow, have you had any periods where you felt back to your usual self, where you could enjoy things and function normally for a while?\n\nPatient: Well, um... there was this one time, yeah. It was a stretch of maybe a few weeks where the heaviness kind of lifted a bit. I was actually getting through my work at the office more normally, answering emails without staring at the screen for an hour first. My manager, Brenda, she missed this huge conference call with the regional directors because her calendar didn't sync. She was so stressed out she brought in a whole box of donuts the next day to apologize to everyone, even though it wasn't her fault. They were from that place on 4th Street, the one that makes those maple bacon bars? I don't really get the appeal of bacon on donuts, honestly. Sweet and salty is fine, but meat on a pastry just feels wrong to me. I prefer just a classic glazed. \n\nBut, um, anyway... yeah, during those few weeks I was working better, but... things still weren't right. Even though my mood was better, I was still hearing that voice by the air vents. And I was still completely sure that the mortgage company and my coworkers were monitoring me. Like, I knew they were watching. So, I wasn't really back to my usual self, I guess. Just... less sad while all the other stuff was still happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I haven't. Honestly, it's all just been happening at the same time. During all those months where I was barely eating anything, barely sleeping, and just feeling like a complete and utter failure... I was also hearing that woman's voice. And I was completely convinced that people were watching me, waiting for me to make a mistake with the house or my job. 

Speaking of my job, they just changed the break room policy again. We used to be able to keep our lunches in the fridge for three days, but now they're saying they're going to throw everything out every single Friday at 3 PM. Which is ridiculous, because sometimes I work late on Fridays and I want my leftover pasta. I make this really good baked ziti with three different kinds of cheese. Ricotta, mozzarella, and parmesan. You have to use whole milk ricotta, though, the skim milk kind gets too watery when you bake it. My grandmother taught me that. She used to make her own pasta from scratch, hanging it all over the kitchen chairs to dry. She had this old hand-crank pasta machine that she brought over from Italy. I tried to use it once, but I got the dough stuck in the rollers and it took me an hour to clean it out with a toothpick. Toothpicks are surprisingly useful, actually. I use them to clean the lint out of my phone's charging port. 

But, um... yeah, no. I haven't had any time where everything was completely normal. It's all just been happening together.

{"candidates": ["F33.2"], "note": "The patient has described a persistent low mood, loss of interest in previously enjoyed activities, feelings of failure and guilt, psychomotor slowing, and unrelenting symptoms over a period of more than two years. There have been only brief periods of slight improvement, but even during those times, the patient experienced ongoing delusions (e.g., hearing voices and believing they were being monitored), indicating that the mood did not fully return to normal. This supports a diagnosis of recurrent depressive disorder, current episode severe with psychotic symptoms (F33.2). No evidence of manic or hypomanic episodes was found, ruling out bipolar disorder. The chronicity and severity of symptoms, along with the presence of psychotic features, differentiate F33.2 from F33.1 (moderate).", "is_final": true}

My manager, Brenda, she missed this huge conference call with the regional directors because her calendar didn't sync. She was so stressed out she brought in a whole box of donuts the next day to apologize to everyone, even though it wasn't her fault. They were from that place on 4th Street, the one that makes those maple bacon bars? I don't really get the appeal of bacon on donuts, honestly. Sweet and salty is fine, but meat on a pastry just feels wrong to me. I prefer just a classic glazed. \n\nBut, um, anyway... yeah, during those few weeks I was working better, but... things still weren't right. Even though my mood was better, I was still hearing that voice by the air vents. And I was still completely sure that the mortgage company and my coworkers were monitoring me. Like, I knew they were watching. So, I wasn't really back to my usual self, I guess. Just... less sad while all the other stuff was still happening.\n\nDoctor: During this current period of feeling down over the past two years, have you had any times where your mood was completely back to normal — where you weren’t hearing voices or feeling like people were monitoring you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Honestly, it's all just been happening at the same time. During all those months where I was barely eating anything, barely sleeping, and just feeling like a complete and utter failure... I was also hearing that woman's voice. And I was completely convinced that people were watching me, waiting for me to make a mistake with the house or my job. \n\nSpeaking of my job, they just changed the break room policy again. We used to be able to keep our lunches in the fridge for three days, but now they're saying they're going to throw everything out every single Friday at 3 PM. Which is ridiculous, because sometimes I work late on Fridays and I want my leftover pasta. I make this really good baked ziti with three different kinds of cheese. Ricotta, mozzarella, and parmesan. You have to use whole milk ricotta, though, the skim milk kind gets too watery when you bake it. My grandmother taught me that. She used to make her own pasta from scratch, hanging it all over the kitchen chairs to dry. She had this old hand-crank pasta machine that she brought over from Italy. I tried to use it once, but I got the dough stuck in the rollers and it took me an hour to clean it out with a toothpick. Toothpicks are surprisingly useful, actually. I use them to clean the lint out of my phone's charging port. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no. I haven't had any time where everything was completely normal. It's all just been happening together."
